みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 9 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 9 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.54％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では9月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、7月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、8月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

モデルによる9 月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、 図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と、高い伸びを示す結果になった。他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究
所による9月14日時点のナウキャスティングが同＋0.98％（年率＋3.99％）と、大幅プラスを予測して
いる。一方、日本経済研究センターが公表した9月のESPフォーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月2
日～9月9日、 回答者37名） における民間予測値平均は前期比年率＋0.25％と、 「みずほGDPナ ウ 」の 予
測値対比で小幅な伸びにとどまっている。7～9月期実質GDP予測値については、データドリブン予測
値と民間コンセンサスが大きく乖離していると言えよう。 
両者の乖離は、景気判断の違いというよりも、GDP統計上のテクニカル要因による部分が大きいと
みられる。4～6月期には、中東情勢悪化を背景に原油・ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、石油化学
製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、いずれも
GDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％） とプ
ラス成長になったものの、成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。反
対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の下
方圧力がかかる可能性がある。多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしているのは、こうしたテ
クニカルな要因が考慮されているためだと考えられる。なお、みずほ総合研究所でも、7～9月期GDP
は横ばい圏の伸びにとどまるとみている。 詳細については、 服部直樹 「年率＋1.4％と1次速報から小幅
に上方修正（4～6月期2次QE）」（2026年9月8日） を参照されたい。  
■ 景気実態は改善方向。今後民間予測値が上方修正される可能性にも留意 
データドリブン予測はテクニカル要因を反映しにくい傾向がある反面、より景気実態に近い動きを
捉えているとみられる。 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては、
9月中旬までに公表された月次経済指標を使って予測値を2回更新した。 図表3では、予測値の更新過程
と各月次経済指標の寄与度を示している2。予測値上昇の主因は、8月の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

通しDI）が高水準となったことである。要因としては、中東危機に起因する供給網混乱の緩和や旺盛
なAI・データセンター関連需要が企業活動を押し上げていることが考えられる。また、鉱工業生産指
数も予測値の押し上げに寄与した。やはり、半導体製造装置などのAI・データセンター関連製品が好
調であるほか、中東情勢悪化の影響で3・4月に落ち込んだ石油化学製品の回復傾向が続いている。 
以上のように、原油やナフサの輸入増、企業の在庫調整といった動きから、GDP統計上の7～9月期
成長率は低い伸びに抑えられるとみている。ただし、「みずほGDPナウ」が示すとおり、足元の景気
実態は4～6月期対比で緩やかな改善傾向にある。総じて企業部門の回復を示す指標が多く、特に鉱工
業生産は8月の生産予測指数（補正値ベース）が前月比＋3.1％となるなど、好調の持続を示唆してい
る。石油化学製品における在庫復元の動きが続く可能性も考慮すれば、民間エコノミストによるGDP
成長率予測値は今後上方修正されるかもしれない。 
次回の「みずほGDPナウ」については、8月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて7～
9月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組むこととし、10月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。中東
情勢悪化やAI・データセンター投資増の影響が入り組む中、ナウキャストによる景気動向の把握はよ
り重要なものとなるだろう。

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

## Document 058: 2026年9月日銀短観

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月日銀短観.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年10 月1 日 全 8 頁

2026 年 9 月日銀短観 
AI 需要で製造業は改善も、先行きはコスト増や金利上昇で業況悪化

経済調査部 エコノミスト 中村 華奈子

[要約] 
 2026 年 9 月日銀短観では、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最近）は＋24％pt（前回差＋
2％pt）に改善した一方、大企業非製造業は＋35％pt（同▲2％pt）へと悪化した。製
造業では、旺盛な AI 関連需要が景況感を押し上げた。非製造業では、燃料費や人件費
などのコスト増が重石となり、 「宿泊・飲食サービス」や「小売」など個人消費に関連
する業種を中心に業況が悪化した。 
 業況判断 DI（先行き）は、大企業製造業で＋21％pt（今回差▲3％pt） 、大企業非製造
業で＋30％pt（同▲5％pt）といずれも悪化が見込まれている。中東情勢に収束の兆し
が見られないなか、原油価格の高止まりに伴うコスト増 への懸念や、金利上昇による
資金調達コスト増大や需要の下押しへの警戒感から、幅広い業種で慎重な見方が広が
った。 
 2026 年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額
は含まない）は前年度比＋7.6％へと上方修正されたが、おおむね例年通りの修正幅だ
った。省力化投資や更新投資、能力増強 投資等への意欲は引き続き強いとみられ、大
企業では製造業・非製造業ともに前年度比 2 桁増の計画が維持されたほか、中小企業
でも、非製造業を中心に前回調査から上方修正された。 
 企業の販売価格の見通しと物価全般の見通しはいずれも高水準にあり、企業の価格設
定行動は今後も積極的な状態が続くとみられる。資金繰り判断 DI（最近）や金融機関
の貸出態度判断 DI（最近）は横ばい圏で推移しており、金融環境はおおむね緩和的な
状態が維持されているといえる。もっとも、借入金利水準判断 DI（最近）は大企業・
中小企業ともに上昇し、先行きも一段の上昇が見込まれるなど、利上げの影響が企業
にも広がりつつある。

### Page 2

1

2 / 8

【業況判断 DI（最近） 】AI関連需要が追い風となる一方、コスト増で非製造業は悪化 
2026 年 9 月の日銀短観では、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最近）は＋24％pt（前回差＋2％
pt） 、大企業非製造業では＋35％pt（同▲2％pt）となった。製造業では、旺盛なAI関連需要が
景況感を押し上げた。非製造業では、燃料費や人件費などのコスト増加が収益の重石となり、
個人消費に関連する業種を中心に業況が悪化した。 
大企業製造業のうち、 「素材業種」の業況判断DI（最近）は前回差＋7％ptだった。原材料コ
スト上昇分の価格転嫁が進展し、「石油・石炭製品」 （同＋27％pt）や「鉄鋼」 （同＋12％pt）
などの業況判断 DI（最近）が上昇したほか、 「窯業・土石製品」 （同＋11％pt）や「非鉄金属」
（同＋9％pt） 、 「化学」 （同＋6％pt）といったAI関連の素材を扱う業種でも業況が改善した。 
「加工業種」の業況判断 DI（最近）は前回差＋2％pt だった。 「生産用機械」 （同＋7％pt）や
「電気機械」 （同＋4％pt）では、半導体関連を中心とする AI 関連需要を追い風に業況が改善し
た。他方、 「食料品」 （同▲6％pt）では原材料・エネルギーコストの上昇が重石となったとみ
られるほか、 「造船・重機等」 （同▲3％pt）や「自動車」 （同▲1％pt）でも業況判断DI（最近）
が低下した。 
大企業非製造業では、「宿泊・飲食サービス」 （前回差▲8％pt）や「小売」 （同▲4％pt）、
「対個人サービス」 （同▲3％pt）など、個人消費に関連する業種で業況判断 DI（最近）が低下
した。燃料費や人件費の上昇に加え、訪日客数の停滞などが景況感を下押ししたとみられる。
また、 「運輸・郵便」 （同▲4％pt）では燃料費の上昇、 「物品賃貸」 （同▲4％pt）では金利上昇
に伴う調達
コストの増加が業況の悪化につながった可能性がある。

【業況判断 DI（先行き）】コスト高や金利上昇への警戒感から幅広い業種で悪化見込み 
大企業製造業の業況判断 DI（先行き）は＋21％pt（今回差▲3％pt） 、大企業非製造業は＋
30％pt（同▲5％pt）と、いずれも悪化が見込まれている。 
大企業製造業では、 「素材業種」が＋15％pt（今回差▲10％pt） 、 「加工業種」が＋23％pt
（同▲2％pt）と素材業種の低下幅が大きい。中東情勢に収束の兆しが見られないなか、原油価
格の高止まりに伴うコスト増や原材料の供給制約に対する警戒感が重石となったとみられる。
一方、加工業種では好調なAI関連需要もあってDIの低下は小幅だった。 
大企業非製造業では、 「物品賃貸」 （今回差▲19％pt） 、 「不動産」 （同▲11％pt）、「 建 設 」（ 同
▲8％pt）など幅広い業種で業況判断 DI（先行き）が低下した。物品賃貸や不動産では、金利
上昇による資金調達コストの上昇や、それに伴う設備・住宅需要の下押しが懸念されたとみら
れる。物価上昇に伴う家計の実質購買力の低下や最低賃金引き上げに伴う人件費負担の増加に
対する懸念も、幅広い業種で慎重な見方が広がった一因と考えられる。

【売上高・経常利益計画】全規模全産業は増収増益も、円安の影響には注意が必要 
2026 年度における全規模全産業の売上高は前年度比＋4.1％の増収、経常利益は同＋2.2％の
増益が見込まれている。2026年度の想定為替レート（円/ドル、全規模・全産業）は154.23 円
であり、足元の水準（158 円/ドル台）よりも円高水準にある。今後、企業の想定為替レートが
円安方向へ修正されると、輸出比率の高い製造業を中心に、売上高や経常利益計画が上方修正
される可能性がある。他方、輸入燃料や原材料への依存度が高い運輸・郵便や食料品などの業
種では、円安に伴うコスト増加を受けて経常利益計画が下方修正されるリスクにも注意が必要
だ。

【需給・価格判断】中小企業では先行きの価格転嫁の進展が見込まれる 
大企業の「国内での製商品・サービス需給判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業で▲1％pt（前回差＋
3％pt） 、非製造業で＋4％pt（同▲2％pt）だった。先行きは、製造業で▲1％pt（今回差±0％
pt） 、非製造業で＋3％pt（同▲1％pt）と見込まれている。 
大企業の「海外での製商品需給判断 DI（最近）」は、 「素材業種」で▲8％pt（前回差±0％
pt） 、 「加工業種」で＋3％pt（同＋3％pt）であった。 「海外での製商品需給判断DI（先行き）」
は、 「素材業種」と「加工業種」のいずれも今回差＋1％pt と改善した。 
大企業の「仕入価格判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業で＋59％pt（前回差▲3％pt） 、非製造業で
＋54％pt（同▲2％pt）であった。先行きでは、製造業が＋55％pt（今回差▲4％pt） 、非製造
業は＋54％pt（同±0％pt）となった。大企業の「販売価格判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業が＋
40％pt（前回差±0％pt） 、非製造業が＋38％pt（同▲2％pt）で あった。先行きは、製造業が
＋40％pt（今回差±0％pt） 、非製造業が＋40％pt（同＋2％pt）となった。 
企業の
交易条件（販売価格判断 DI と仕入価格判断 DI の差）を確認すると、中小企業では製
造業、非製造業ともに先行きの仕入価格判断DI の変化幅を販売価格判断 DI の上昇幅が上回っ
ており、価格転嫁の進展が見込まれる。 
企業の物価見通し（全規模全産業）を見ると、販売価格の見通しと物価全般の見通しはいず
れも高水準にある。企業はこれまでのコスト増を持続的な物価上昇圧力として認識している可
能性があり、今後も企業の価格設定行動を後押しするとみられる。

【設備投資計画】26 年度は前年度比＋7.6％の見込み 
2026 年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額は含ま
ない）は前年度比＋7.6％と、おおむね例年通りの修正幅であった。省力化投資や更新投資に
加え、能力増強等への需要は引き続き強いとみられ、大企業では製造業・非製造業ともに前年
度比 2 桁増の計画が維持されたほか、中小企業でも、非製造業を中心に前回調査から上方修正
された。

【雇用判断】雇用の不足感は中小企業を中心に一段と強まる見込み 
雇用の過不足感を示す「雇用人員判断 DI」 （最近）は、大企業製造業で▲22％pt（前回差▲
1％pt）、大企業非製造業で▲37％pt（同▲1％pt）だった。中小企業では、製造業で▲32 ％pt
（同▲4％pt） 、非製造業で▲47％pt（同▲1％pt）となった。 
先行きは、大企業製造業で▲ 27％pt（今回差▲5％pt）、大企業非製造業で▲ 37％pt（同±
0％pt）、中小企業製造業で▲37％pt（同▲5％pt）、中小企業非製造業で▲50％pt（同▲3％pt）
と見込まれている。中小企業を中心に雇用の不足感は一段と強まる見込みだ。

図表11：雇用人員判断DI

【企業金融】緩和的な金融環境はおおむね維持も、借入金利が上昇 
資金繰り判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業で＋13％pt（前回差＋1％pt） 、中小企業全産業で
＋8％pt（同±0％pt）となった。また、金融機関の貸出態度判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業
で＋12％pt（前回差▲1％pt） 、中小企業全産業で＋12％pt（同±0％pt）と、横ばい圏で推移
した。金融環境はおおむね緩和的な状態が維持されているといえる。 
他方、借入金利水準判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業で＋65％pt（前回差＋6％pt） 、中小企
業全産業で＋70％pt（同＋9％pt）と上昇した。先行きについても、大企業全産業 で＋67％pt
（今回差＋2％pt） 、中小企業全産業で＋74％pt（同＋4％pt）と一段の上昇が見込まれている。
資金繰りへの影響は現時点では限定的であるものの、利上げの進展を受けて、中小企業を中心
に資金調達コストの上昇圧力は今後も強まる見込みだ。

### Page 1

欣銓 (3264 TT)  Buy

晶圓大廠新訂單逐步向上，建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ：欣銓 2026 年資本支出轉向積極擴張，主要來自與策
略客戶已確定之 ASIC 合作案。欣銓 1H26 在取得不動產、廠房
及設備的現金流出達新台幣 70.07 億元，較 1H25 的 18.65 億元
大幅增加約 51.4 億元。預期 2H26 則隨龍潭廠機台陸續交付並
完成數據驗證後，進入產能爬坡期，營收逐步向上。在排除匯
率波動前提下 ， 獲利亦預計維持逐季成長的趨勢 。 預估欣銓2026
年稅後 EPS 9.94 元。龍潭廠新專案是 2026~2027 年最重要的增
長引擎 。 公司與策略夥伴(晶圓廠 )合作的 ASIC 測試專案 07/2026
開始明顯增加，而旗下全智湖口新廠，也於 3Q26 正式進入矽光
子相關新產品的量產階段，都將帶動公司中長期營收獲利向上，
因此建議欣銓 Buy ，目標價 322 元 (2027 年 PER24X) 。  
2Q26 稅後 EPS 2.36 元： AI 相關需求佳和通訊相關訂單維持強
勁。欣銓 2Q26 營收為 45.34 億元， QoQ+13.81% 。營收依應用
別分析，通訊 Communication/Connectivity 佔 33.4% ；車用 /安控
(ATV/Security) 佔 18.2% ；射頻晶片 (RF IC) 佔 12.2% ；微處理器
(General MCU) 佔 8.5% ； Storage 佔 10.8% ； PC/Consumers 佔
9.9% ；Memory 佔 5.4% ；其他 1.6% 。毛利率的部份， 2Q26 毛利
率為 40.17% ，增加 1.81 個百分點。 2Q26 稅後 EPS 2.36 元。  
預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.73 元：龍潭新廠歷經 1H26 客戶驗證與
進場裝機後，已於 07/2026 起正式進入量產階段，帶動欣銓
08/2026 營收增加至 17.94 億元， MoM+1.57% ， YoY+46.57% 。
此廠區主要與晶圓代工廠 (Foundry) 合作，針對  AI ASIC 提供
測試服務。這批高階機台目前的稼動率極高，幾乎處於滿載狀
態，其量產將顯著貢獻 3Q26 營收，也會讓晶圓代工業務的營收
佔比在 3Q26 逐漸放大。此外，在傳統應用領域如車用 IC 及  
General MCU 方面 ， 由於客戶端在疫後的庫存已降至合理水位 ，
需求正逐步回升。預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.73 元。

2 群益投顧 
  欣銓 ESG ：  
欣銓積極推動  ESG （環境、社會、公司治理）相關政策與行動，並於官方
網站設有專屬  ESG 頁面與歷年永續報告書，公開揭露永續發展成果與目
標。  
環境（ Environmental ）  
•持續推動再生能源與減碳：延續加入  RE100 的承諾，積極推動節能減碳
與再生能源採購，並簽署 SBTi （科學基礎減碳目標倡議）承諾，強化減碳
目標與國際接軌。  
•循環經濟新提案：攜手供應鏈夥伴推動循環箱再進化，落實資源再利用與
減廢。  
•生物多樣性與生態尊重：持續舉辦淨灘、認養友善田等活動，強調尊重生
態、友善健康。  
社會（ Social ）  
•同仁參與與文化深化：舉辦「 ESG Award 」專案競賽，鼓勵員工提出永續點
子與專案，涵蓋綠色製造、責任供應鏈、多元包容職場、人才培育、關懷弱
勢等五大方向。  
• 員工關懷與職場環境：持續改善員工宿舍環境，提升同仁生活品質。  
•社會公益：贊助在地傳統藝文活動、捐助弱勢團體，並與產業園區合作舉
辦移工籃球賽，促進多元共融。  
公司治理（ Governance ）  
•資訊透明與永續報告：發行永續報告書，持續自主揭露非財務資訊，強調
資訊透明與溝通成效。  
•國際認證與標準：完成 RBA VAP 8.0 改版訓練，強化人權、環保等國際標
準合規與超越法規要求。  
•永續獎項肯定：榮獲「英國標準協會（ BSI ）ESG 永續發展領航獎」及「台
灣企業永續獎（ TCSA ）－永續報告獎金獎」，肯定公司在永續發展、資訊
揭露與社會責任的表現。  
攜手合作夥伴擴大永續影響力，推動以大帶小、標竿學習。展現穩健落實永
續目標與全球標準接軌的實力。  
◼CDP 氣候問卷榮獲  A-List 最高肯定 ： 公司在2025 年  CDP 全球環境評
比中，從全球逾  22,100 家企業脫穎而出，「氣候變遷」問卷榮獲 A 級領
導等級，「水安全」蟬聯  A- 領導等級肯定。  
◼ SBTi 近程目標通過驗證：公司設定的近程減碳目標已於 04/2026 正式通
過科學基礎減量目標倡議（ SBTi ）官方驗證。  
◼連續  9 年榮獲「公司治理評鑑前  5% 」的殊榮。  
◼龍潭廠榮獲「 2026 台灣建築永續獎金獎」肯定。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 166: A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader (2026-10-02).pdf

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #interestrates #businessconditions #credit
A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:43 September 18 2026 Share
Business investment is now in the driver’s seat on growth, implying credit growth is a less
reliable guide to financial conditions more broadly.
Strong business investment, autonomous from government or central bank policy, has filled the gap
left by slower growth in public demand, and changed the narrative around the growth cycle. This has

implications for how we should think about financial conditions, and specifically business credit

### Page 2

Historically, much of the commentary on the growth cycle in Australia has been policy-driven. The RBA
sets the cash rate, which affects housing and mortgage markets, and so consumer spending. Business
investment was seen as largely downstream of a consumer-driven domestic demand cycle, on the logic
that businesses will not invest if demand is not there.

There were exceptions to this, most notably the rise of China and associated mining investment boom.
The pervasive effects of this boom and its unwind were systematically underestimated. Also not fully
appreciated at the time was the deadening effect of risk aversion and weak investment appetite in the
wake of the GFC. But these factors mostly played out over a longer horizon, and the shorter-run rates–
consumer nexus tended to be more in focus.

Another policy-driven narrative became more prominent in recent years, with public demand as the
swing variable. Government infrastructure projects and the growth in the care economy were driving
growth in domestic demand. Initially we worried that when the infrastructure projects ended and the
expansion in the care economy matured, the handover would be ‘shaky’ and growth momentum would
weaken further.

Fortunately, that did not happen. Developments in the private sector, autonomous from domestic policy,
have recently become a key driver and filled the gap as public sector demand growth slowed. In many
respects, the data centre boom and associated renewables spend is rewriting the narrative, and our
recent forecast revisions reflect this.

This shift has several implications for how we should think about financial conditions and specifically
how business credit growth fits into any assessment of financial conditions.

First, business investment is not that correlated with short-term interest rates. This was true in
the post-GFC period of slow growth, when hurdle rates stayed high even though interest rates were
unusually low. It will also be true in the upswing. The hyperscalers and other funders that are driving
the data centre boom are essentially indifferent to cost. They do not seem to care that they are funding
themselves at much wider spreads than similarly rated non-tech firms. Nor do they seem to care about
cost to build. They just want to get it done as fast as possible. But if their activities are impervious to
growth.
Typically, business investment is only loosely correlated with interest rates, and business credit
growth is in turn only loosely correlated with new investment. Business balance sheets strengthened
in the wake of the pandemic, so there might be scope for further increases in leverage. The pre-
pandemic period of low growth, inflation and interest rates is in any case not the relevant period of
comparison for where we are now.
If something is essentially impervious to interest rates, then it cannot be an indicator of financial
conditions more broadly. There is a difference between a claim that policy is restrictive but needs to
be even tighter because of a positive shock, and a claim that policy is not tight at all. These are
important considerations for how we should interpret developments in business credit.

### Page 3

interest rates and spreads, they cannot guide views on how the economy is responding to interest
rates.

Second, business investment is only loosely correlated with business credit quarter to quarter or
year to year. A stylised description of many businesses’ strategies is to use debt funding to acquire
assets or other companies, but use retained earnings to fund new investment. Short-run fluctuations in
business credit growth might therefore provide weak or misleading signals about investment or activity
more broadly. Moreover, if businesses’ funding mix changes, even medium-term shifts in business
credit growth might have little implication for underlying activity.

Third, balance sheets matter. Businesses came out of COVID cashed up, in part because of the fiscal
support they received during the pandemic. Business leverage – whether measured as debt relative to
financial assets broadly or debt to cash plus deposits – dipped below even the low post-GFC average.
It has since returned to that average but might have scope to increase further. This would not
necessarily mean credit supply has eased. Rather, the population of borrowers has effectively changed
and become stronger.

Fourth, and related to the previous point, the comparison period matters. We must recall that the
period between the GFC and the pandemic was the anomaly – low growth, low inflation, low
investment – and extremely low interest rates. The global structure of interest rates is likely to be higher
now and into the future, because of the AI boom, the US fiscal situation and the end of the post-GFC
inflation undershoot. But this is not news, and it is not necessarily a sign of a change in financial
conditions more recently.

This point is particularly salient for total credit growth as an indicator of financial conditions. Even a
return to inflation around or above target, rather than consistently below as in the 2010s, means that
average nominal credit growth rates from that period are not representative of “normal”. Nominal
growth in the economy was slower, and nominal credit growth is downstream from that. Moreover,
many of the factors that made the post-GFC period unusual – the weak risk appetite, regulatory
adjustments and balance sheet repair – would be expected to weigh on credit growth especially
heavily. There is also a cyclical element here: more (real) credit growth might not be saying anything
about credit supply, and so “financial conditions”. Rather, stronger growth makes for stronger borrowers
that are more willing to demand credit and more qualified to access it.

All of this suggests that observers should be cautious in how they interpret business credit conditions.
If something is essentially impervious to interest rates, then it cannot be an indicator of
financial conditions more broadly. To be fair, the hyperscalers are issuing bonds, not borrowing from
the domestic banking system and being recorded in business credit. To the extent that current business
investment and credit growth are being driven by the data centre boom’s broader spillovers to other
sectors, though, that provides almost no signal about the tightness of monetary policy.

### Page 4

Related articles
This is important given the apparent tendency of some recent RBA and other commentary to conflate a
shock with a reason to revise the neutral rate higher. For example, in one recent speech, it was argued
that, because AI was a smaller shock for Australia than some other countries (debateable), long yields
would rise by less than elsewhere. This in turn would result in a lower exchange rate than otherwise,
necessitating higher interest rates. But the exchange rate depreciation is part of the transmission of
that differential response to different-sized shocks. It is not an independent outcome requiring that
interest rates need to be higher, actually. In the face of a positive shock, yes, policy rates would need to
be higher than if the shock had not occurred. But being a lesser beneficiary of that shock is not a
heads-I-hike-tails-your-rates-go-up double-whammy.

This framing is even more curious, when one considers that the RBA never took any signal from the
USD selloff earlier in the year. That selloff had nothing to do with conditions in Australia, but it surely
lifted the AUD exchange rate at the margin – at least against some trading partners – and therefore
was slightly disinflationary.

The broader lesson here is not that this time is different, but rather “last time” – the period between the
GFC and the pandemic – was different. It has also taken a while to normalise from the pandemic itself,
especially where balance sheets and financial behaviour are concerned. If we fail to understand this
recovery away from the post-GFC era, or the implications of more fleeting shocks for financial
behaviour, we risk misconstruing where the economy might be headed and where policy settings need
to be to achieve desired outcomes. The strength of business credit does not have the same
implications as similarly strong household credit would.

The boom in data centres and renewables is reshaping Australia’s economic narrative and needs to be
a consideration in policy settings. But care must be taken to avoid overstating its implications for
financial conditions. There are many good reasons to think that the long-run average for the global
structure of interest rates (and so neutral rates) will be higher in future than pre-pandemic. Current
strong growth in business credit is not necessarily one of them.

图1：瓶颈随工作流程向下转移——清晰度，继而验证，再而控制
代理式人工智能降低了编程成本，但这并不意味着软件开发变得廉价。
部署自主型人工智能并不会缩短交付链条，而是重塑它。
企业必须更清晰、更迅速
地决定其需求；工程部门必须在不降低质量的前提下验证更大量的产出；运营部门
必须识别代理在生产中可能采取的行动以及相应的控制措施。本文探讨了这三个要
点。
源自奥利弗·怀曼分析（示意图）。

© Oliver Wyman

瓶颈随着工作流程向下游移动而转移。
哪三章节相互关联？
加速生成减少了约束条件。
瓶颈虽然转移了但并未消失，反而暴露了下一个瓶颈。同时，也移除了一
个先前免费运作的过滤器。当建筑成本高昂时，成本因素禁止了低价值功能的创建。现在成本降低了
，必须将限制设计在内。如果这样不做，软件将变得更加复杂，运行成本更高，使用难度也更大。在
新模式下，稀缺资源变成了上游的清晰度、中游的验证以及下游的控制。投资应随之转移。
促进最终步骤进入生产。
在此，第3章——交付与运营的问题不再是一个代理能否行动，而是它应该被赋
予何种权限。本章划定了实际的操作边界。根据交付系统，领导者可以将本地、可逆的数据平面操作（如
准备补丁、重新运行测试或重启服务）委托给人工智能。同时，他们可以要求人类监督生产策略、审批关
卡和其他控制平面的决策。这只有在平台使每个操作都可见、可追溯且可逆时才有效。
在编写一行代码之前就开始。
当产出第一章——需求与意图时，一个模糊的简报会变得更加昂贵。风险
在于，现在可能会快速构建出错误的东西。我们主张将需求视为一组受管理的资产，这些资产清晰、可
测试、有版本控制，并在团队间进行协调，同时将最终决策权留给业务方。实际问题在于，组织能否足
够清晰地陈述其需求，并足够快速地做出决策，以便代理能够行动。
多年来，软件交付一直围绕工作流链条中间的稀缺资源进行组织。
工程能力是瓶颈。现在情况已经改
变。智能代理（Agentic AI）意味着代码可以更快地产生，但这并不意味着整个链条平行加速。相反，
压力向上游转移至意图的清晰度，而工作则向下游转移至验证、发布和运营控制。接下来的三章按照工
作流的顺序排列，反映了每个章节为下一个章节创造条件的现实。
它关注的是构建本身。
这正是代理式AI最显著的地方，也是最容易将更多输出与更多价值混淆的地方。
随着生成的加速，审查、测试和领域判断的过程成为瓶颈。因此，本章将注意力从代码量转移到可验证
性上。这意味着要给代理分配可以可靠检查的工作，使质量门禁持续且难以绕过，并衡量实际通过管道
的是什么，而不是产生了多少代码。

广的节奏。
初次阅读时，我们建议按顺序阅读这三章。
这样做是有道理的，因为需求创造了需要工程验证的规范，
而工程则创造了交付必须发布且安全运行的变化。之后，每一章都独立存在，作为决策指南。它们共同
回答了一个CIO的问题：当代理使生产加速时，工作流程中必须做出哪些改变，才能让速度带来可靠的
软件，而不是更快的返工和风险？

代理测试是构建：变成

需求与意图：当代码免费时，
价值去向何方？
人工智能让编写可用软件变得容易多了。
然而，仍然困难的是决定要构建什么——并且要精确且快速地
完成，以保持代理的运作。虽然代码曾经是瓶颈，但现在这种稀缺性已经转向了清晰、一致的简报，以
及能够快速做出决策以采取行动的高层支持。在大型公司里，这种情况只会变得更难，因为不同团队越
来越多地使用人工智能参与草拟过程，此时可能会同时收到数百个请求。这些请求不能相互矛盾，并且
每个请求都必须足够完整，以防止人工智能快速构建出错误的东西。撰写一份好的简报曾经是一门手艺
。现在，它必须像管理一个活生生的产品一样进行管理。

© Oliver Wyman

图4：CEO们认为工作流程再造的收益不在于工具，而在于流程本身
奥利弗·惠悦 2026 年 CEO 与科技领袖代理型 AI 与 IT 并行调研，2026 年 7 月
CEO们认为代理式AI的主要收益来源是哪里，%的受访者
重塑工作方式
并管理我们的数据
大致相等
购买和部署最佳工具与模型
企业必须将实际投资用于运用人工智能和合适的工具，以确保需求理解准确无误，而不仅仅是更快
地生成代码。
将需求视为一个包含版本历史的“活”的真相来源：跨功能更新需求、协调矛盾，并
记录关键决策的制定过程。与业务部门保持最终确认，并重建业务与IT围绕频繁、良好决策协同工
作的方式。人工智能所能提供的价值越来越取决于企业能否明确其需求并迅速做出必要决策。更快
的代码生成并不能解决模糊或冲突的需求。

Veizaga、S. Y. Shin和L. C. Briand，“自然语言需求中的自动化气味检测与推荐”，IEEE软件工程汇刊第50卷第4期，2024年
，第695–720页
我们对这个方向充满信心：约束已从构建软件转变为决定要构建什么。
我们对工具本身则更为谨
慎。学术研究表明，软件现在可以自动检查需求中的矛盾，但用于管理需求（或简报）的端到端
工具仍处于萌芽阶段，目前还没有针对需求是否契合项目总体目标的大规模、独立评估方法。

人工智能的代码生成能力正以大型组织难以协调需求并做出决策的速度在增长。
五个挑战将瓶颈问题提
前了。前两个挑战关乎意图的质量和一致性。接下来的两个挑战涉及权限、治理和决策速度。第五个挑
战则关乎决定不构建什么的纪律。
人工智能几小时就能草拟需求和原型，但它无法领会人们通过会议、经验和日常协作所建立的真实世界
、非正式的背景。
此外，应用程序的文档可能过时，无论是编写规范还是代码本身。
请要求您的团队识别出哪些现行要求相互矛盾，哪些不完整。
在我们的针对130名受访者的CIO调查中
，只有15%的人表示他们的组织能在一天工作时间内常规性地回答该问题。另外74%的人表示这需要大
量努力，而11%的人完全无法回答。如果您的组织无法快速提供可靠的答案，则在允许AI大规模构建之
前，请先解决该能力问题。
如果目标、约束、依赖关系、数据定义和先前决策没有被明确说明或保持更新，代理将自行填补这些空
白。
否则，下游的不同团队和代理可能会做出不同的假设，从而产生大量看似合理但既无法整合也未能
反映预期结果的代码。
当决策跟不上编码时会发生什么？
员工仍然可以使用人工智能来更快地起草需求，但在建设开始之前，他们还应该使用它来提出、丰富
和测试这些需求的潜在影响。

### Page 11

© Oliver Wyman
11

2. 一个要求在脱离语境时可能很清晰，但在语境中却可能是错误的。
4. 简要质量成为成功执行的关键瓶颈。
通常情况下，需求说明（briefs）没有得到妥善管理：可能没有版本历史记录，或者没有可从中生成软
件的主源文件（这与其说是关于代理（agent）被授予的自主权水平的问题，不如说是关于规格作为产品
（spec-as-product）成熟度的问题）。
人工智能或许能够组织并起草需求，但判断他们所起草的内容是否正确——并正式批准——这不是可以
委托的事情。
这项责任始终属于企业，因为最终企业要对结果负责。4 任何由此产生的延误都会增加成
本：代理人要么等待，从而降低对其投资的回报，要么基于其自我生成的假设继续工作。这会导致偏差
，并在后期制造更多工作。
一项要求单独阅读时清晰，但在引入整个组织时仍可能失败——因为它可能与现有的数据模型、系统、
流程、政策、法规或其他正在进行中的变更产生冲突。
现在，软件可以一次性检查整个产品组合中要求
之间的矛盾：独立的学术测试表明，人工智能可以可靠地标记不完整或模糊的要求，而早期研究将此扩
展到冲突或相互依赖的要求。² 这解决了人工审核在规模化处理时难以应对的问题，但前提是公司首先
就人工智能进行检查的通用定义达成一致。
但检测矛盾相对容易。
更困难的一步，正如受控研究所示，是在编写任何代码之前，将每项要求与现有
系统、规则和依赖关系进行核对。这样做可以在错误蔓延之前发现它们。3 但尽管自动化检查可能能够
证明软件符合需求说明，但它们无法证明该需求说明是否表达了业务的需求或期望。
2 I.
达里夫 (Darif), 尼乌 (F. Niu), 阿布德拉蒂夫 (M. Abdellatif), 布里亚德 (L. C. Briand), 拉梅什 (S. Ramesh) 和 阿迪
斯南 (A. Adiththan), “利用大型语言模型自动检测需求依赖”, 2026年2月.  
然而，今后必须确保简报保持完整和一致，并且业务决策得到相应体现。
公司自动化规模越大，简报就
越需要规范性——只有当生成工具成熟并被信赖时，全面实施才能获得回报。5 如果成功做到这一点，
那么需求管理将停止制造更多文书工作，转而成为对一项宝贵资产的管理。
3.
编程速度更快并不一定意味着决策速度更快。人类仍然需要对需求进
行确认。

### Page 12

© Oliver Wyman
12

6 Anthropic，《AI原生SDLC手册》，2026年8月。
5.
低价代码移除了过去曾用于阻止低价值工作的过滤器。
当建筑成本高昂时，成本充当了一个粗略但有效的过滤器：那些无法证明值得团队投入时间的功能永远
不会被开发。
随着生成一个功能的外边际成本趋近于零，这个过滤器就消失了。风险并非开发者闲置，
而是相反：大量功能被开发出来，仅仅因为它们“可以被”开发，而非因为任何人需要它们。每一个发
布的功能都带来了生成成本无法削减的成本。它必须被测试、保障、文档化、集成，并在产品存在期间
得到维护，同时它还要与那些重要的功能争夺用户的注意力。
该工具正分裂为两大阵营。
成熟的规范平台如Jama Connect侧重于受控规范、可追溯性和受监管环境，
而新的规范驱动型工具——如AWS Kiro、GitHub Spec Kit和BMAD-METHOD——则更直接地利用规范来协
调AI辅助开发。该类软件仍在发展演进中，因此CIO们应通过具体用例进行试点，而非过早地将其中任何
一种作为标准采用：合适的工具取决于该简报主要是受控记录，还是协调跨交付代理的操作性输入。
结果是，软件变得越来越复杂，运行成本越来越高，同时使用难度也越来越大。
防止这种情况需要的是
需求端的规范，而非工程端的：每个请求在进入简报前必须达到明确的价值门槛；能显示哪些功能实际被
采用的使用数据；以及拥有否决权和权限淘汰不再被使用的功能的指定负责人。如果一个组织无法阐明某
个功能为何存在，就不应该因为相关人员有能力就允许他们去构建它。

图7展示了标准方法：将约定的条款、数据结构及其含义的上下文统一存放在受控位置，然后为每个
需求和代理使用该参照。
单一的、企业范围的定义并非总是适用，因此批准的变体应说明其适用范围
。在此，所有权、版本历史和变更控制至关重要，因为更改一个定义或数据结构可能同时影响多个需
求和代理行为，产生连锁反应。
许多跨团队冲突源于对术语的不同理解。
“客户”、“活跃账户”、“收入”，甚至“完成”这些词
在财务、销售和运营部门中各自可能包含三四种定义。在人员和代理能够基于相同要求工作时之前，
组织需要为其核心业务概念建立共同的语言。这意味着不仅要就相关术语的含义达成一致，还要就它
们在数据中的表示方式达成一致：标识符、字段、关系和规则。但尽管人们通常可以通过对话和经验
弥合此类不一致性；代理却做不到。如果名称、定义和数据结构不同，代理可能会每次对相同的请求
做出不同的解读。

键业务术语及其定义。
直到这些定义被同意并集中管理——技术人员称之为“共享语义层”——每个团队，以及现在每个AI
代理，都基于自己的版本进行工作。
不同的团队只有在模块无法契合时才会注意到矛盾。
一旦基础定义达成一致并确立，接下来的、更复杂的挑战就是解决优先级冲突。
利益相关者可能
对事实达成一致，但仍希望有不同的
同意关键条款并将其集中管理，有助于治理，并能实现自动跨项目冲突检查。
企业范围内的统一性——无
论人类还是人工智能，都基
于同一含义进行工作。
变更受控，所有权与变更历
史确保定义演进而不破坏一
致性。
自动冲突检测：需求和代理
都根据相同的定义和上下文
进行检查。
因此，他们应从小的范围开始，先就最重要的少数几个术语达成一致，将这些术语集中管理，并以此作
为所有需求和代理人的参考依据。
正是这种治理机制使得自动冲突检查成为可能，因为软件只有在每个
术语都有一个可供对照的受控定义时才能标记出冲突：要么尽可能采用单一的企业释义，要么是一套明
确的、具有清晰适用规则的、特定于上下文的释义集。这种工作无法现成购买，因此需要投入工作。它
可能会挖出先前隐藏在格式不匹配的电子表格中的开放性定义争议，但它将是所有其他工作的基础。

结果，但共享的定义和评分模型仍然无法解决相互冲突的激励。
相反，这需要指定领域所有者、由高级
管理人员进行仲裁以及在建设开始前的战略选择。如果没有指定负责人，工作就会停滞不前，或者嗓门
最大者获胜。这两种结果都无法证明战略的存在。
• 一人所有，一份权威版本 • 设定决策权责与响应时间 • 代理行动前进行验证（人工确认） •
监控偏差并重新验证
在一家最近的金融服务客户那里，一个代理式工作流原型在两天内就准备好了，但部署被推迟了十周
，因为首席运营官办公室、IT部门和财务部门就应快速推出一个范围狭窄的解决方案，还是等到生产
集成、控制所有权和效益案例都完全达成一致后再启动争论不休。客户发现打破那僵局的最佳方法是
在首席运营官办公室任命一位高级赞助人作为
人工智能驱动的快速原型设计，通常被称为“氛围编码”，提供了一种更快地集中优先级的方法。
团队
无需在冗长、文字繁多的规范中就竞争性需求进行争论，而是可以将流程知识、数据、规则、约束和假
设输入到智能体中，并在数小时内将不同选项转化为原型。通过与用户和业务负责人快速测试这些选项
，可以迅速发现优先级冲突之处，并使权衡取舍变得具体化：哪些有效，哪些无效，以及业务愿意放弃
什么。然后，通过短反馈循环，将选定选项转化为包含明确用例、验收标准和测试用例的更丰富的规范
。一旦达成一致，后续的代码生成就变得更加自动化，也更容易验证。

### Page 16

© Oliver Wyman
16

将简报视为关键资产进行管理
执行准备能力测试
1.
哪些主动需求存在冲突或不完整，我们能在一天内查明吗？  
2. 当业务领域希望达成不同结果时，由谁打破僵局？  
CIO无法单独解决决策速度问题。
有五个问题可以告诉任何高管，组织是否已做好准备：
如果那些答案缺失了，那么再投资代码生成工具也无法解决瓶颈问题。
责任决策者，并引入一个由一页决策备忘录和五工作日响应目标支持的每周决策论坛。
通过采取这些措
施，客户能够将后续审批周期从周缩短至天。
鉴于这将影响多少决策，需要采用分层渐进的方法：可逆、低影响的选项应与AI的工作节奏保持一致；
跨
领域的权衡、受监管的决策和不可逆转的承诺则应遵循更审慎的节奏——每种情况都应明确响应时间预
期，以便将速度设计到流程中，而不是偶然发生。这样一来，决策延迟就可以成为另一个可量化的性能
目标。
随着代码生成变得商品化，持久性的优势正转向那些能够比竞争对手更快地将意图转化为可靠、可运
行的软件的公司。
然而，变得稀缺的是：撰写一份完整、无冲突且指定了负责人（named owner）的需
求文档（brief）的能力；在一天内就能知道该文档是否包含不一致或遗漏；以及在交付的节奏下，成
功促使领导层做出决策。
建立和保护知识产权需要围绕塑造它们的领导决策建立运营控制机制。
将简报视为一个共享数据库，而
不是一个文档文件夹。它应有明确的归属、一个权威版本、可追溯的决策、代理人行动前的核查，以及
对重大决策设定响应时间。

图9：适用于行动摘要的四项控制措施

撤销不合理的权力
掌握自己想要什么的组织将占据优势。
随着代码生成日益商品化，不久的将来，每个竞争对手都将运行基于相似模型的可比代理。
差异将取
决于那些代理被要求构建什么。我们的建议依然不变：在扩大生成规模之前，先投资于前期规划，并
且对于不构建什么要像对于构建什么一样审慎。然后实施受控定义、具有命名所有者的单一权威版本
、代理行动前的检查，以及与风险和可逆性相匹配的决策权。这些要素都无法购买；它们代表了业务
和IT共同决策方式的转变，其进展体现在决策所需时间的长短。
那些清楚自己想要什么并且能够决定其代理人员工作节奏的公司，将从人工智能中获取最大价值。
那种
花了两天时间制作的原型却等了十周才获批的模式，是企业现在就应该着手消除的类型，而自主交付（ag
entic delivery）的应用仍处于快速扩张阶段。否则，企业会发现，缓慢决策的成本会随着每一位新代理
人员的加入而不断叠加。
来源：奥利弗·怀曼分析。
这些控制结合了“像管理资产一样管理”的原则，以及对保持审批人为化并在人工智能行动前进行检查的
独立研究。漂移信号旨在跟踪您自己的工作，而不是作为已证实的行业基准；目前仍然没有衡量需求完整性的大规模标准。

7 Hitzig、Massenkoff、Lyubich、Heller和McCrory，《代理编码与持续的专业技能回报》，Anthropic，2026年6月16日。8 Demirer
、Musolff和Yang，《编写代码与发布代码：跨代人工智能编码工具的生产力效应》，麻省理工学院斯隆研究论文（待发表），SSRN，
2026年9月6日修订。
此观点聚焦于交付链的中间环节：软件是如何被构建和接受的。
随着代码生产的成本降低，投资重心应从生成更多代码转向那些稀缺的资源：领域知识、确保产品
发布后仍能存活的评审能力，以及实施无法绕过的质量门槛的能力。
给予代理过多独立性可能导致
代码一致性降低、IT债务增加，以及产品增长速度超过用户或维护者吸收能力：任何通过管道的功
能仍需经过评审、保障和在其生命周期内持续维护。
构建可靠的工程运营模式
企业应将可自动验证的工作交给代理，其余工作则保留给人员。
不要因为依赖就等到最后才进
行验证。
将代理系统导向可靠的产出。
人类设定方向、护栏、要求和验收标准。代理负责执行、生成、测
试、验证合规性并部署。
自主AI在构建阶段最为明显，这也是其影响最常被夸大的地方。
Anthropic对40万次Claude Code会话的
研究发现，大部分收益是由领域能力而非AI本身带来的。7 随着它们沿着交付链向下移动，收益也会减
少：在提交代码方面的约+240%提升，在项目层面降至约+80%，而在实际发布的版本中仅为+30%。8 因此
，虽然编写代码正变得廉价，但将其转化为可靠且真正能交付的软件仍然很困难。对于CIO而言，当数量
变得廉价时，问题在于应该将重点放在哪里，以便额外的产出能创造价值而非债务。

### Page 21

© Oliver Wyman
21

单线比率：代码生成量与审查测试能力之比。
大约27%有代码生成项目的组织表示，代码生成速度超过了审查和测试能力，而41%的CIO估计，有5%或更
多的AI辅助代码缺陷会流入生产环境。
与此同时，审查和测试能力在过去一年中是五个投资领域中排名
最后的。
如果代码生成量持续上升而相关控制保持不变，企业实质上就是在制造债务。
在这种情况下，企业最
好暂停代码生成的推广，重新平衡支出——将其投入到更多的审查和测试能力上——以避免处理量超
出控制能力。
提交量增长了240%，但实际发布的版本增幅约为30%。
生
产力的提升是真实的——其中大部分并未成功发布到生产
环境。
持续的警觉性——人们可能会感到疲劳，并且有可能过度信任疲惫的员工。
相反，应将其作为一
项贯穿整个建造过程的、工具支持的持续检查，纳入流程中。同时，要注重测量监控，以便能够
量化实际进展速度，而不仅仅是估算。

每个因素将在后续段落中阐述；人才计划将从CIO/CHRO的角度（C6）展开。
将规划与执行分离可以提高代理人的交付能力：在Claude Code会话中，目前人类大约做出70%的规划决
策，而代理人处理大约80%的执行决策。
9 这种清晰的区分——即编码代理当前发挥优势的领域——表明
，能够将规划与执行分离可以有效地、安全地带来生产力的提升。
9 Hitzig、Massenkoff、Lyubich、Heller和McCrory，《代理编码与持续的专业技能回报》，Anthropic，2026年6月16日。

### Page 24

© Oliver Wyman

3

验证必须持续进行，而不是留到最后：如前所述，提交的数量远比发布快得多。
如果没有相应的检
查机制，这种额外的吞吐量可能会变成未来的清理工作和安全风险。大约有22.7%由人工智能引入的
缺陷会存活到仓库的最新版本。11 在长期迭代任务中，代理编写的代码也会持续退化：在大多数运
行中变得更混乱、更臃肿，并且生成的代码明显比人类维护的代码冗长。12
此外，风险不仅限于代理编写的代码。
它们还会以机器速度导入依赖项，同时，Mythos级能力正使攻击
者审计继承库以寻找可利用漏洞的成本更低。向疲惫的审查者工作负载中增加更多检查并不能快速解决
问题；因此，验证必须与吞吐量同步扩展。13
因此，测试需要转变为一种持续的能力，涉及：基于规范的测试生成；
合成测试数据；自愈脚本；以及
一个编码化的“完成定义”，该定义能自动检查架构、合规性以及依赖项/SBOM。而测试人员则应将精力
投入到质量策略上——基于风险而非编写模板来决定测试内容。最后，也或许是最重要的是：问责制纪
律需要保持至高无上，需要指定一名负责人来拥有价值提升带来的收益，以及承担成本/风险带来的代价
。这个人反过来就成为了最重要的控制点，既受到激励又被赋权，以提供持续且一致的利益。
实际上，这种分离能走多远取决于什么可以被验证。
高容量、低复杂度的编码之所以便宜又快，正是因
为它容易检查。有了合适的规范，代理可以生成一套强大的自动化测试，用于功能和非功能属性。对更
复杂工作的验证越好，公司对最终产出的信心就越强。这反过来又支持给予代理更大的自主权，从而强
化整个周期的效率。强大的验证也有助于弥补模型能力的参差不齐，否则可能会对不可靠的输出产生虚
假的信心。
工作节奏将从冲刺转变为流动：既然一个小团队在几小时内就能完成过去一个大型团队需要数天才能完
成的工作，那么两周的冲刺期相比以往来说就不再那么合理了。
相反，高级产品架构师会组建团队（
或称为“Pod”），而开发人员则将时间用于指导那些无需遵循固定时间表工作的代理。
10 卡尔帕蒂/塞昆尼亚，2026年。
11 《AI繁荣背后的债务》，2026年。  
12 奥尔兰斯基等，《SlopCodeBench：基准测试编码代理在长期迭代任务中的退化情况》，2026年5月修订版。

© Oliver Wyman

4

15 Oliver Wyman 2026 年 CEO 与科技领袖平行调研：关于自主人工智能与 IT，时间：2026 年 7 月
知识之外，顶尖人才将带来需要经验积累才能掌握的技能：人工智能并非取代人类人才的需求。
它正将
招聘的重点从知识转向技能。这种模式并不新鲜：它曾随着转向更复杂编程语言和工具而出现。编码能
力并非消失，而是改变了形态：清理未分配内存块的能力不再是差异化因素，而结构化问题解决、系统
思维、基于抽象和封装的架构设计、勤奋以及沟通能力将继续保持重要，即使人类团队越来越多地监督
代理并拥有代码的“完成定义”，而非亲自编写代码。超过三分之二的CIO表示，寻找和重新培训人才
以利用代理是他们最大的人才限制，而只有3%的人提到“替换或减少工程师”。15 但一个更难回答的
问题是，当初级工程师无法像传统上那样接触基础知识以构建技能时，如何培训他们。答案是学徒制和
特定代理的拥有权相结合，初级工程师向指定的导师辩护代理的工作，同时进行治理、风险管理、质量
保证及相关主题的人工智能素养培训。留住优秀员工也变得日益关键。这意味着不仅要确保工程师掌握
正确的技能，还要让他们从行政事务和琐事中解放出来，将时间用于高价值的问题解决。
简而言之：组织设计和初级人才梯队应是首要决策，而非人力资源的临时想法。

真正的问题，与其说是你能产出多少代理输出，不如说是你能坚持多少，以及任何人实际上需要多少。
还
有，五年后你是否仍会有能理解这些的员工。
每个控制面板都会显示您的工程师在产出更多代码，但这数字可能具有误导性。
原始代码的规模目前很
廉价且容易；但剩下的困难之处，以及区分劣质软件与优质软件的关键，在于评审能力、验证以及领域
判断。这些都不会随着代理输出的增加而自动扩展。将代码生成视为基础设施，通过设置关卡和基于工
具的验证，而非监督。根据验证和衡量工作容易程度来决定使用代理的位置，并依据贵公司的实际成果
而非代理供应商的市场营销数据来做决策。然后，将释放出的投资投入到领域专业知识、评审能力以及
初级人才管道中。
专注于代码生成的公司，在更远的未来会遇到瓶颈。

### Page 30

© Oliver Wyman
30

数据平面和控制平面
上下文
巴恩斯、加莱布与哈桑，《代理式CI/CD与权限转移》，2026年。
代理人能否安全地运输和操
作软件，还是仅仅编写代码
更快？
在合适的条件下，如今的代理机构可以安全地承担数据平面工作。
将控制平面自主权交给它们则尚待验
证，且那里的错误可能会以机器速度扩散。对于一家运行在传统基础设施的大型企业而言，实际问题在
于权限：要转移什么、如何治理它，以及组织是否能在事后重建代理机构所做的工作。
虽然代码开发可能正变得像商品一样普遍，但安全地交付和运营软件仍然充满挑战，并且持续获得的关
注远远不够。
主要平台已经支持在持续集成和持续部署（CI/CD）——即构建和部署代码的流水线——
上进行代理式工作。现在，代理也开始承担站点可靠性工程（SRE）的任务，确保系统稳定运行、响应
故障事件以及管理基础设施。
这个问题已超越了代理人是否能在技术上执行这些任务。
真正的问题在于，代理人可能会做出什么样的
操作决策，在何种限制条件下，以及如果出错会采取何种补救措施。这种区别将数据平面（如本地化工
作，如补丁和重跑）与控制平面（规则：配置、部署策略、审批关卡）区分开来。
数据平面关乎本地化、可逆的操作：生成补丁、重新运行测试、重启服务。
控制平面是生产规
则手册：管道配置、部署策略和审批关卡。

调查数据显示了有界自主性的需求。
人们感觉到CEO愿意授予的自主性与CIO目前部署的自主性之
间存在脱节：大多数CEO倾向于设定严格、预设的限制，而大多数CIO则报告在需要人工批准的层面
上运行。
这些公司在数据平面上赋予代理机构自主权的大小，应取决于其下方的交付系统：发布流程和记录
必须可靠，团队需要一种共享的方式来判定发布的紧急程度。
代理将运行的平台应可观测，并在代
理引入之前就准备好。架构本身必须在设计上包含爆炸半径，因为这些故障模式可能迅速蔓延。SR
E 纪律也需要延伸至代理，将交付稳定性与采用率一同衡量，以便激励措施能够强化重要成果。
我们相信从有限的自主性开始会更好。
企业应让代理承担数据平面工作，但将控制平面权限保留
在人类手中。
≤ A3 · 人类批准

18 Oliver Wyman 2026 年 CEO 与科技领袖关于自主人工智能和 IT 的平行调查，2026 年 7 月
控制平面治理应易于测试。
如果代理能够更改部署策略或清除审批关卡，且无需人工控制且无任何记
录，则控制平面不受治理。如果事后无法重建是哪个代理更改了什么以及其权限归属，情况亦然。在
建立这些控制措施之前，应暂停控制平面的授权；构建治理和审计追踪是必须首先完成的关键工作。
话虽如此，目前大多数公司距离这一目标还有相当差距，仅有10%的公司能够按需提供每个生产代理的
所有者、范围、成本和最后认证日期。58%的公司拥有部分这些属性，而29%的公司完全没有中央注册
记录。18
快速修复场景向我们展示了该划定界限之处。
当关键服务出现故障时，代理可以映射风险并准备修复方
案。然而，在非正常时间窗口外部署变更属于控制平面操作，应保持人为控制和记录。这也是人们最想
跳过这些步骤、将修复工作交给代理的时刻——这正是区分的重要性所在。
第十七项证据：CEO们倾向于有限自主权，而部署的代理仍然依赖人工批准

现代人工智能模型本质上具有不可预测性，且并非为提供保障而构建。
它们可能以多种方式失效：模
型可能在提示阶段被误导而违背自身规则，或获得超出预期的权限。一个代理的错误也可能级联影响
到其他代理——升级的速度可能比人类反应更快。
最安全的系统仍然是那种受周边治理支持的有限自主系统。
即便是迄今为止发布的最具自主性导向的供
应商操作手册也坚持这一原则：其自主维护循环仍然保持检测的确定性，通过违规严重程度限制代理可
以执行的操作，并且在最高自主性级别下仅允许代理提出行动建议——一个等待审核的拉取请求，或是
一个预先批准的运行手册。23

精心设计可以控制“爆炸范围”。
解耦和模块化的架构、赋予代理最小化访问权限、限制其工具访问以
及要求对重大操作进行审批，都是有效的控制方法。检测也必须自动化，因为仅靠人工审核无法跟上节奏

人工智能本身可用于通过映射依赖关系和交叉引用服务级遥测数据来协助进行
这些分类，但其目标和激励措施必须与执行交付工作的代理保持界限分明、相互独立。一旦所有相关方就
分类达成一致并将其融入流程后，自主权可根据发布的关键性而有所不同，并随着代理生态系统首先在高
容量、低风险工作上证明自身价值而扩展。
一个代理所能达到的安全程度，永远取决于其运行的平台。
在安全方面，开发者平台和底层基础设施
的重要性，超过了代理的智能程度。在这种情况下，代理的准备工作取决于四个能力。动作必须作为
安全的、带有内嵌策略检查的应用程序编程接口（API）提供；代理需要关于资产、依赖关系和所有权
完整可靠的上下文数据；堆栈需要带有每个代理的数字身份的内嵌控制；以及命名问责制，以及适当
的生命周期管理、互操作性和包含推理成本监控的上下文管理。21 没有这四个要素，堆栈就没有准备
好进行安全的扩展。

## Document 182: Allianz_2026-09-17_How the AI supercycle is reshaping global interest rates_f60e61d1

Source PDF: all report pdf/Allianz_2026-09-17_How the AI supercycle is reshaping global interest rates_f60e61d1.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

In Summary 
• The AI supercycle will pull long -term yields down in both the US and Europe , but for 
very different reasons and by very different paths. We model this across five transmission 
channels – three macro (neutral rate, inflation expectations, fiscal collateral) and two 
micro (AI -driven duration supply and crowding -out) – applied to an AI upside and 
downside scenario. Over a 10-year horizon the net effect is around −50bp for both regions 
in the upside scenario (roughly +5pp of cumulated US potential growth, +1.6pp for the 
eurozone), and a prolonged, smaller drag in the downside (−2.5pp and −1.3pp 
respectively). The US result depends on AI producti vity actually materializing to keep its 
fiscal trajectory intact; the eurozone gets to the same place even without the AI dividend, 
since its fiscal position doesn't need repairing in the first place. The US is the levered, 
volatile expression of the AI trade; Europe is the safer one. 
• The market has already priced the AI productivity story for the US, but not the fiscal 
relief that should come with it. The USD 5y1y forward has moved from ~3.0% to 4.9% since 
mid-2025 some 150bp above our median neutral -rate estimate and 90bp above the top  
of our estimate range, and more than the full +67bp our model assigns to the Ai enhanced 
neutral-rate range over the next decade. What isn't priced at all is the fiscal collateral 
improvement, worth −85bp to the US 10Y by the end of the horizon. Either th e market 
doesn't believe AI will repair the fiscal trajectory, or it's pricing a less favorable version of 
the same story with higher AI -driven job displacement pushing up social protection and 
unemployment spending enough to offset the tax-revenue gain. 
• Fiscal collateral – the extent to which a stronger economy makes a government's debt 
easier to repay – is what actually decides the outcome, in both scenarios and both 
regions. The transatlantic rates converging seems counterintuitive at first view as Europe's 
growth impulse is a third the size of the US. But its fiscal position benefits more, because 
the improvement lands on a stable debt path rather than the expansionary US pat h. For 
every point of growth AI delivers, Europe's fiscal position improves abou t 1.6 times more 
than that of the US. 
• The way AI interacts with interest rate isn't a straight path, and its shape differs on each 
side of the Atlantic.  Both curves rise well above their eventual resting point before 
reversing, the US 10Y's round trip is 135bp (versus 80bp for the eurozone) even though the 
net 10 -year effect is only −50bp for both. The peak trade also looks different in each 
market: a butterfly in the US (buy the belly, sell the wings, as front-loaded micro effects hit 
the wings while macro effects lag into the belly) versus a flattener in Europe. By the end of 
the period both curves converge to a similar, milder flattening.

How the AI supercycle is reshaping global interest

### Page 2

AI is no longer only a sector story, but a macro-scale claim on the economy. 
Artificial intelligence (AI) has become a major macroeconomic force. AI investment will pass USD1tn globally in 
2026, of which more than USD500bn in the US alone . That is 1.8% of US GDP and 0.9% of world GDP, on a path to 
2.8% and 1.4% respectively by 2028.  In terms of growth, tech and data center activity will represent 36%-50% of US 
year-on-year growth this year. Globally, the direct AI investment impulse is worth roughly 10% of global growth. 
From the financial markets side,  AI has mostly been examined through the lens of  equities (sector analysis , 
valuation, concentration risk), or private credit  (risk profile and risk pricing of AI investments types (see our latest 
reports here linked here). This paper asks how the AI supercycle transmits into interest rates .. 
US and eurozone vary in the level of macro impact, but share similar profile 
We model the effect against a baseline without AI effects, measuring the difference versus an AI  upside scenario 
and one in which AI shifts to the downside. We use several published estimates to derive scenarios for the impact 
on potential growth, trend productivity and trend employment. We define an  upside scenario  with cumulated 
potential growth gains of +4.94pp in the US and +1.60pp in the eurozone; in the downside we assume -2.47pp in the 
US and -1.35pp in the eurozone. The US macro scenario shows much greater sensitivity to AI, while Europe has less 
to gain in terms of productivity . The pattern is similar for inflation, where in the upside scenario the US face s a 
stronger supply- and energy-driven uptick, before higher productivity gains take over and reverse the AI impact into 
a disinflationary trend. Inversely in the negative scenario, the negative AI demand shock brings US inflation further 
down than it does in the eurozone (Table 1). 
Table 1: Macroeconomic scenarios

Sources: Allianz Research

Our framework rests on five major transmission channels 
We identify two major channels of transmission: 1) a macroeconomic channel through potential growth, inflation 
dynamics and the fiscal position (plus a spillover effect for the eurozone), and 2) a microeconomic channel through 
the changes AI financing brings to the structure of fixed income markets. The latter has gained importance recently 
as market participants have discussed the possibility that AI debt issuance was competing with US government debt 
and therefore contributing to driving up yields. The macro channels capture the changes in the way long-term rates 
discount economic variable s. The micro channels c apture how the AI funding model (market size, self-funded vs 
debt issuance) affects the price of duration. Macro and micro channels work on different lags, and their effects can 
change sign over our 10-year forecast horizon. Generally, the macro channels affect the expected rate component 
of long-term rates, while micro channels act on the term premium. The exception is the fiscal position which, despite 
being a macro channel, affects the term premium component of long term government bond yields.  
1) Neutral rate r*  is the real policy rate at which the economy neither overheats nor stalls. It is the level the 
expected path of short rates is anchored to. If AI raises trend productivity, capital earns more, firms want to 
invest more, and the neutral rate balancing saving against investment rises. A lasting productivity gain 
therefore lifts r*  which feeds through the entire yield curve. It has  a stronger effect at the front end as it 
transmits through expected short rates, and productivity gains have a long-term mean reverting trend. The 
effect is inverted in case AI would fail to produce productivity gains in an economy facing a debt overhang 
from AI investments.  
2) For inflation expectations we use an adaptive expectation approach where the estimation windows adjust 
with previous estimation errors. As AI has a strong supply impact at first, we get upside push in expectations

### Page 3

3

that fades with time when productivity gains take over . As the phase in becomes smoother, the change in 
expectation is less pronounced to the downside. These factors also influence the expected rate component 
of government bond yields. 
3) Fiscal collateral is defining the fundamental value  of sovereign bonds as discounted claims on future 
primary surpluses. The channel captures its change in response to an AI-driven up- or downside productivity 
shock. A productivity shock has an asymmetric effect: tax revenues improving the fiscal position are elastic 
to GDP, while the largest spending lines (pensions, benefits, debt service) are sticky.  
4) Duration supply  is a market -microstructure model tracking AI capex as a generator of new duration, 
through both long -dated IG bonds and pay -fixed swap structures in private credit. The curve effect sits at 
the long end  as 65% the supply lands beyond 10 years. The swap  spread effect  depends on fixed-rate 
issuance, which  cheapens the sovereign against swaps  while p ay-fixed hedging from priv ate credit, 
enrichens it. Duration supply affects the term premium, it is more pronounced at the longer end of the curve. 
5) Crowding Out is a sector -level displacement model affecting long -term rates via the swap-market. 
Financial institutions issue fixed-rate bonds and then ask for floating, receiving fixed in swaps. That receive-
fixed leg is how financial issuance moves duration out of the sovereign market. AI borrowers sit on the 
opposite side of the swap curve, paying fixed rather than receiving it . So both channels lift swap yields 
against the sovereign: one adds fixed -payer supply, the other removes fixed -receiver demand . Like 
duration supply, crowding-out affects the term premium at the longer end of the curve. 
6) Spillover Effect is added for the eurozone, acting mainly at the long end, where global term premia and 
rate levels remain structurally correlated and anchored to the US benchmark. It captures how AI effects 
propagate to global fixed income markets via the US Treasury market, even where AI exposur e is limited.

The AI super cycle is a bull market for duration, but it is not a directional trade 
Our results show that AI is a bull market for duration in both scenarios, but the path matters. In the upside scenario, 
AI lowers long-term rates by around 50bp over a 10-year horizon in both the US and the eurozone. In the downside 
scenario, rates are also held down for a prolonged period. In both cases, however, yields rise before they fall: the 
US 10Y makes a 135bp round trip in the upside and 72bp in the downside, against 80bp and 41bp for the eurozone. 
The AI effect on rates is therefore not a long-term directional trade, the right timing is essential. The timing depends 
on which channels dominate when, how long they take to act and where on the curve they bite hardest. That makes 
the shape of the curve as important as the yield level for positioning (Figure 1). 
Figure 1: Net AI effect on the 10Y yield – Upside and Downside scenarios 
in basis points

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

The end points for the US and the eurozone are remarkably close, even though the two start from very different 
positions in the AI race. The fiscal collateral channel explains why. Europe gets only a moderate growth impulse, 
but it lands on a stable fiscal position, which improves the value of its fiscal collateral significantly. The US gets a 
much larger growth impulse, yet the gain is partly offset by continued fiscal expansion over the next 10 years (we 
use the CBO forecast of debt/GDP rising 20pp over the next 10 years ). With a stable debt path, the increase in US 
fiscal collateral  value would subtract 125bp rather than 85 bp, lifting the total effect to -90bp and leaving the 
eurozone far behind. The transatlantic AI rates convergence is thus a late-cycle outcome: at the peak, the AI effect 
is more than twice as large in the US as in the eurozone.

The US curve rotates between the peak and the end of period due to micro effects 
At the peak of the upside scenario, the US curve shifts up, but with the middle of the curve (belly) holding up better: 
the 2s30s slope barely moves while 2s5s flattens 13bp and 5s30s steepens 12bp. The peak AI trade on the US curve 
is therefore a butterfly (buy the belly, sell the wings). In the eurozone the peak AI effect on the curve is a flattener 
instead: 2s30s −20bp, with the 2Y up 51bp against the 30Y up 31bp. This shape difference is driven by the stronger 
and front-loaded US micro channels, set against macro channels that arrive with a lag. In addition to the time lag, 
macro channels (neutral rate, inflation expectations) act mainly at the short end, while the micro channels hit the 
long end because AI issuance is long -dated. The 5Y sits in between: it absorbs little of the duration -supply and 
crowding-out pressure, yet is long enough to benefit from the improving fiscal collateral effect on its term premium. 
By the end of the period, both curves flatten to a similar degree  as fiscal collateral value becomes the dominant 
force, pulling the belly and the long end down while the 2Y is barely affected  (US 2s30s −41bp, EA −3 0bp) In the

Microeconomic channels and inflation move first, the fiscal channel decides the end point 
Having looked at the  way AI effect the  changes in yield levels and the influences the shape of the curve s, we now 
turn to the decomposition of the moves : which channels drives the yield and curve dynamics, how much each 
contributes and when. The sequence is the same in both scenarios: micro channels  and inflation expectations drive 
rates early on, while the fiscal channel and the neutral rate r* only kick in later.  
In the upside scenario, the peak arrives when productivity gains lift r* while inflation expectations are still elevated 
and the micro channels are still at work but before higher growth has significantly fed through to tax revenues rising 
the value of the fiscal collateral. For the US at the peak in 2032, r* alone contributes +67bp against −18bp from the 
fiscal channel. By the end point the picture reverses: the fiscal channel contributes −85bp, r* slows down to +42bp 
and inflation expectations flip to −18bp as the disinflationary effect of productivity gains sets in. Europe follows the 
same sequence at roughly half the amplitude, reflecting its lower sensitivity to AI.  
In the downside scenario, rates first move up, driven by inflation expectations on the back of AI investment and 
energy demand. In the US, the micro channels add another 20 -30bp from AI-related issuance. The tipping point is 
in 2031: productivity gains fail to materialize and the debt overhang starts weighing on growth, which later erodes 
tax revenues, increases government spending and reduces value of the fiscal collateral. In the eurozone the micro 
channels are negligible, but the spillover from US rates matters. 
The greater US sensitivity also shows up in the gross channel activity , i.e. the sum of all channel contributions in 
absolute terms. In the upside it amounts to 158bp in the US against 78bp in Europe for a broadly similar final 
outcome. In other words, much more happens under the surface in the US, but most of it cancels out: only 30% of the 
US channel activity is left once the channels are netted against each other, compared with 58% in Europe. The AI 
journey is therefore likely to be bumpier for US rates than for eurozone rates, even if both end up in a similar place. 
The AI effect should therefore translate into relatively higher US rates volatility  (Figure 4 & 5). 
Figure 4: Channel contributions to the 10Y  - Upside and Downside scenario 
in basis points

### Page 7

7

Figure 5: Channel contribution at every point of the curve: peak and terminal (basis points) 
In basis points

The fiscal channel has the last word – and the US needs the AI dividend more than Europe 
We find no transatlantic divide in the long-term AI effect on rates. At the end of the period the US and eurozone 10Y 
effects are only a few basis points apart in both scenarios. But the path to that point differs materially in level, drivers 
and timing. The convergence of the end points is attributable almost entirely to fiscal channel. Fiscal collateral 
contributes −85bp to the US terminal effect in the upside and −46bp to the eurozone, in both cases more than the 
channels working in the opposite direction, principally the neutral rate r* effect. 
The mechanism is the productivity beta of government debt. A positive productivity shock raises tax revenues by 
more than the GDP gain, so the present value of future primary surpluses improves and the bondholder is effectively 
long AI productivity. Europe is disadvantaged here, as it tax revenues are less sensitive to GDP (also because AI 
growth is tilted versus US and less taxable by European governments) but its spending is also more sticky, as Europe 
is more exposed to negative labor market effects caused by AI. On the other hand, the fiscal trajectory of Europe is 
more favorable, with a rather stable debt to GDP ratio projection of 86% for the next 10 years. Over the same time 
period, the CBO sees US debt growing to 20pp of GDP. 
What differs between the two markets is the debt path on which that improvement lands: deteriorating in the US, 
stable in the eurozone. The US is therefore the more dependent of the two on the AI productivity call actually being 
realized, as it needs the additional revenue to sustain its current expansionary fiscal stance, whereas the eurozone 
would maintain its debt trajectory even without the AI dividend.  
On our calibration, Europe’s fiscal-collateral beta is 1.6× the US per point of growth delivered, notwithstanding the 
more favorable US elasticities (higher revenue elasticity, lower spending elasticity). The difference lies in the 
denominator: a smaller debt stock (EUR1 4tn against USD 40tn), a lower starting yield, a lower baseline real rate

and a flat baseline debt path against a deteriorating one, with the CBO projecting US debt rising 20pp of GDP by

advantage would widen further (Figure 6).

The neutral rate: bigger in the US, more decisive in Europe 
The r* channel is larger in the US but more decisive in Europe. In the US upside it is the biggest single positive 
contribution at both moments, + 67bp at the 2032 peak and +42bp at the 2036 end point, but it sits inside 158bp of 
gross channel activity and is therefore largely netted away by the other channels. In the eurozone it is effectively 
the only positive channel left at the end of the period, at +15bp. Both paths rise with a delay, starting two to three 
years from now. The US path runs much higher but also mean -reverts more strongly later on, so the gap to the 
eurozone, which follows a smoother path, narrows over time. In the downside the paths are less pronounced and 
the two regions are closer together: the absolute impact is stronger in the US, while the eurozone shows the higher 
sensitivity relative to its own starting point.

AI issuance is affecting the US treasury market, the euro market is untouched for now 
The micro channels are a US story: the US dominates AI-related debt issuance and duration supply. In the eurozone 
the effect is still small, with EUR-denominated AI issuance accounting for around 1% of the corporate benchmark

The market prices the AI productivity gain, but not the fiscal relief that should come with it 
In the US, the rates market has already fully priced the productivity leg of the AI story. The 5y1y forward swap has 
climbed from 3.0% in mid-2025 to 4.9%, against a median neutral-rate estimate of 3.4% and range of 2.7-4.0%, which 
puts the market -implied neutral rate some 1 50bp above the median and 90bp above the top of the band. Our AI 
models would add +67bp to current estimates pushing the range to 3.3-4.67% – a range the market already exceeds. 
What is missing from the current A I narrative pricing is the other side of the same story: the fiscal collateral 
improvement that these productivity gains should bring, worth −85bp on the US 10Y  over the next ten years. That 
leg arrives with a much longer lag, as it takes a visible turn in tax receipts to shift the narrative (Figure 9). We would 
therefore read the current level as a temporary mispricing rather than a challenge to the fiscal leg of the AI story. 
The front-loading of the productivity channel builds up correction potential.

## Document 200: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

• UN General Assembly: President Trump will be meeting with seve -

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROZONE / EUROPEAN UNION
ECB speakers temper hawkish tone even as incoming data points
to resilience. Several Governing Council members confirmed the
ECB might need to increase interest rates further but signaled they
saw no haste to do so (including President Lagarde and Vice-Pre -

sident Vujcic). (We foresee a final 25 bps hike in December).
In July, exports rose 1.2% m/m while imports fell 0.4% m/m; ex -
ports to the US rebounded (+6.0% m/m), offset by a similar decline
in sales to China. Industrial production fell less than expected in
July (-0.1% m/m, -0.3% m/m for manufacturing), held back by lower
motor vehicle output, while computer and electronic equipment
bounced back sharply. Forward-looking PMI and EC surveys signal

further improvement ahead. The ECB wage tracker points to a pick-
up in wage growth in 2027 after stabilising in H2 2026, with the
base-wage index reaching 2.9% y/y in June 2027—though not si -
gnaling “second-round effects” from the ongoing inflation spike.
Consumer inflation expectations (ECB survey) edged up in August,
however, with the median up 0.1 pp both for one year (3.0%) and
five-year (2.5%) ahead. The ECB’s Supervisory Banking Statistics re -

vealed a strong banking system as of end-Q2 2026. Capital ratios
remain at record highs (16% in Q2 2026 for CET1), whilst non-per -
forming loan (NPL) ratios were at record lows (2.2%). Return on
Equity reached a peak not seen since 2015 at 10.73, thanks to a
cost-to-income ratio at its lowest level since 2015 and stable net
interest margins.
Transatlantic relations: Two surprise rapprochements. On Friday,
President Trump announced the US had reached an agreement with

Denmark and Greenland giving the US “permanent control over
security and other needs” including by restricting the rights of non-
NATO members in the territory. Denmark confirmed the deal should
be signed soon. Also unexpectedly, President Von der Leyen used
her SOTEU speech to offer Canada “associate membership” in the
EU, an undefined status that does not exist in the EU treaties and
would require unanimous approval from the 27 member states.
France: Higher fiscal deficit and lower growth forecasts

overshadow positive news on business starts. Prime Minister S.
Lecornu announced that the budget deficit is expected to reach 5.4%
of GDP in 2026, versus 5% targeted and 5.1% in 2025. This overrun
stems mainly from interest payments, fuel subsidies, and below-
forecast growth. The draft budget for 2027 is expected to target
a deficit of 5% of GDP . Government spending would be completely
frozen (excluding interest payments and defence), and the fiscal

effort would aim to be more evenly distributed than in the past.
Meanwhile, the Banque de France (BdF) has lowered its growth
forecast from 0.5% to 0.4% for 2026 and left unchanged its forecast
for 2027 at 0.9%. The OAT-bund spread topped 100 bps on Friday,
its highest since 2012. More positively, business creations rose
16% y/y in August and 12% y/y YTD. The strongest growth was
seen in the information and communications sector (+57% y/y;
Ytd +46% y/y) and business services (+15% y/y; Ytd +19% y/y).

According to the BdF, business insolvencies increased by 4.6% y/y
after +5.1% y/y in June. They are declining in construction (-2.4%
y/y), stabilising in retail, but continuing to rise in certain service
sectors. According to Altarès, late payments fell to their lowest
level in three years. Wage growth accelerated to 2.4% y/y in Q2
from 2.1% y/y in Q1, mainly driven by the increase of the minimum
wage in June.
Germany: Confidence and producer price inflation rise to new

highs. The current economic situation index rose by 14 pts to its
highest level since May 2023 (at -47.1 in the ZEW survey for Sep -
tember. The Economic expectations index rose by 0.5 pts to 34.7,
its highest level since February 2026. Improvements were seen
across all sectors, with only construction and retail remaining be -
low their pre-Iran war levels. Wholesale prices rose by 6.8% y/y in
August (vs. +5.3% in July) due to energy prices, while PPI grew by

4.6% y/y (vs. +3.0% in July and +4.1% expected). Prices for interme -
diate goods rose by 6.1% (from 5.4%) due to a surge in metal prices.
UNITED KINGDOM
A hawkish BOE hold as inflation picks up and activity data reas -
sures. The Bank of England left the Bank Rate unchanged at 3,75%
(6-3 vote), as expected, but shifted its tone to suggest readiness
for a conditional 25bp 'insurance' hike to 4% in November. More
unexpectedly, the BOE announced significant quantitative tighte -

ning (QT) relief with a new Gilt divestment schedule through 2034
that features a pause in active sales until April 2027, direct sales
to the Treasury’s debt management office (instead of to the mar -
ket), and a cessation of sales for bonds maturing after 2049. Gilts
rallied on the news. Headline CPI accelerated to 3.1% y/y in August
(vs. 2.9% in July), meeting market expectations, primarily driven by
rising transport and fuel costs, notably diesel (+8.0% m/m). Core

CPI remained stable at 2.6% y/y. Retail sales volumes unexpecte -
dly rebounded to 0.5% m/m in August (2.4% y/y), despite a drop in
fuel sales. Unemployment held steady at 4.9% in July for the four -
th consecutive month (vs. 5.0% expected). Wage growth accelerated
slightly to 3.5% in the 3 months to July, driven by the public sector
(+6.7%).
UNITED STATES
Kevin Warsh’s Fed delivers. The Fed convincingly set aside doubts
regarding its seriousness about restoring price stability by not only

raising the Fed funds target range by 25bp to 3.75-4.00% (as widely
expected), but doing so unanimously, and with strong ownership
by Chair Warsh, who hawkishly described the move as “removing
a dose of accommodation”. 16 (out of 19) FOMC members now ex -
pect another hike by year-end, and 8 expect another to be neces -
sary thereafter. The FOMC statement and Summary of Economic
projections both expressed confidence in the strength of US eco -
nomy, with not a single member seeing risks tilted to the downside

(a first since 2017). The Committee foresees inflation returning to
its 2% target before 2029, with unemployment falling to 4.1% and
GDP growth revised higher and remaining above trend through

2028, i.e., immaculate disinflation. We expect at least two more rate

regulatory framework for cryptocurrencies and stablecoins and was
seen as a booster to their expansion.
JAPAN
An insufficiently hawkish rate hike as reflation continues. The
Bank of Japan (BOJ) raised its benchmark interest rate by 25 bps to
1.25%, a level not seen since 1995. The move had been expected,
and Governor Ueda tried to sound hawkish at the press conference,

stressing the focus had now shifted to preventing above-
target inflation. However, the lack of unanimity of the decision
disappointed markets (2 members appointed by Prime Minister
Takaichi dissented). The currency weakened markedly following the
decision, to 157 per dollar. Headline CPI was stable at 1.9% y/y in
August, as was core CPI excluding fresh food and fuel, holding at its
fastest pace in three months and pointing to relatively persistent

underlying demand-driven price pressures. As such, we expect the
BOJ to continue to tighten monetary policy to 1.75% by March 2027.
Exports rose 19.3% in August, topping market consensus, supported
by robust demand for AI-related chips. Industrial production fell
0.2% m/m in July, defying estimates of a 0.1% rise and marking
its first decline since March. Machinery orders remained strong,
increasing 11.2% y/y, despite a volatile m/m decline. The capacity

utilisation index rose 0.5% m/m to its highest level since December
2023. PM Takaichi has proposed a temporary food tax cut (from 8%
to 1%) through 2029. Despite government plans to avoid additional
borrowing, the reform faces market skepticism regarding long-term
fiscal discipline and internal party dissent.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Czech Republic: Monetary policy on hold as expected at 3.75%.
The Central bank remains the most hawkish in the region and has

floated the possibility of a rate hike by year-end.
ASIA
China: A continued K-shaped growth. August activity data confirmed
the wide divergence between solid growth of external demand-
driven manufacturing sectors and sluggish growth of sectors
dependent on domestic demand. Industrial growth accelerated to
+5.2% y/y from +4.5% in July, notably supported by semiconductor
production (+20.6% y/y). The contraction in production of refined
products eased in August (-6.9% y/y vs. -16.8% in June-July),

following the recent rebound in Chinese crude oil imports.
Meanwhile, growth in services continued to slow (+4.1% y/y vs.
+4.3% in July). Household spending barely increased in August, as
suggested by weak retail sales (-0.4% y/y in volume), the continued
decline in bank loans to households (-1.6% y/y) and falling housing
sales (-16.2% y/y in volume). Total investment continued to contract
(-7.2% y/y in nominal terms in the first eight months of 2026).

Nevertheless, the Yuan hit a four-year high ahead of a Trump-Xi
meeting next week.
Rest of Asia: Inflation reacceleration in several economies. In India,
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.8% y/y in August from 4.5% in July,
with food inflation rising to 6% and core inflation at 4.2%. Inflation
is no longer confined to food and transport. With the US monetary
tightening, expectations of rise in policy rates by the end of the
year have increased, although the timing remains dependent on oil

prices and downward pressure on the rupee. In Malaysia, August
CPI at 1.9% y/y (vs. 1.8% in July) remains benign despite energy
price pressures. Elsewhere in SEA, inflation is mixed: Thailand at
2.5% (vs. 1.9% in July), Philippines almost stable at 6.1%, Indonesia
at 3.2% (vs. 2.9%) and Vietnam at 4.9% (vs. 4.5%).
LATIN AMERICA
Argentina: Second estimates of real GDP for Q2 confirm the
contraction (-0.6% q/q). All components of domestic demand fell,

especially private consumption (-2.4%). The contraction would have
been worse without the large positive contribution of net exports
(+1.6 pp). In y/y terms, growth decelerated to only 1.4%.
Brazil: The Selic rate was cut by 25 basis points to 13.75%. The
Central bank keeps a “meeting-by-meeting” prudential approach
to preserve its flexibility. Three weeks before the first round of
the presidential election (October 4), the Copom avoids giving any

strong signal that could be interpreted as a political stance.
ENERGY
Stabilisation at high levels: Brent oil prices remain above 100$/b
but stabilised at a slightly lower level than last week, on hopes of
a new diplomatic round in Iran and expectations of a rapid resto -
ration of the Saudi East-West pipeline. However, physical crude
markets remain severely dislocated (Brent physical/future spread is
above 20$/b against 3$/b on average in August) and diesel crack

spreads remain near their highs (above 90 $/b in Europe and
above 110 $/b in the US) as market tensions are stronger than
for crude. On Friday, President Macron called a special G7 Summit
to discuss additional release of Strategic Petroleum Reserves. Eu -
ropean gas prices were almost stable during the week (77 €/MWh
on Monday 21st). EU storage reached a better than expected 68.8%
of full capacity (September 17 th). In Asia, JKM continues to rise

(+7.5% w/w) on the back of unusually hot temperatures in Japan.

## Document 243: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row

pressures

September 2026: Marked increase in inflation,
to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Understanding the balance
ﬁlm industry a for larger AI
chip (Part I)
應該是時候了解 AI ⼤晶⽚抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜產業鏈
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
SEP 28 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
Executive summary
雖然  Balance Film 產業還在初始冒芽階段，但當 AI GPU ， TPU ， ASIC 越來

越⼤顆， ABF 載板及 RDL 處理層數越來越厚，越來越多不同熱膨脹係數晶⽚及
材料被封裝在⼀起，防翹曲技術及抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜就越來越重要。當未來 10
年， AI 算⼒數據中⼼的投資將受限於可⽤資⾦，可⽤電⼒，可⽤⼟地，可⽤晶
⽚產能，除了發展困難度超⾼的衛星軌道數據中⼼，提供更⾼算⼒ / 更低耗能
（單位⾯積算⼒耗能⽐較，增加每單位數據中⼼⾯積的 GW 算⼒）的⼤型 AI 訓
練及推理晶⽚的騰籠換⿃原廠升級（舊機櫃換成新機櫃 + 光通訊  scale
out/scale across ），可能反⽽是⼀個可⾏的⽅向。
What is the balance ﬁlm?
Balance Film （抗翹曲應⼒平衡膜）在先進封裝領域中，是⼀種專⾨⽤來「抵
消」異質整合製程中因熱膨脹係數（ CTE ， Coeﬃcient of Thermal
Expansion ）不匹配所產⽣之應⼒，進⽽抑制載板與晶圓翹曲（ Warpage ）
的關鍵材料。
在進⾏  CoWoS, FOPLP （⾯板級扇出型封裝）或玻璃基板（ Glass

Substrate ）等⼤尺⼨封裝製程時，矽晶粒、矽 / 玻璃中介層， ABF 載板、玻璃
基板（ Glass core substrate) 、銅線路（ RDL ）以及環氧樹脂（ EMC ）的膨
脹與收縮率各不相同。經過⾼溫製程冷卻後，這種應⼒失衡會導致嚴重的翹曲
變形，造成後續微影對位偏移或基板破裂。 Balance Film 透過貼合或塗佈於

### Page 2

基板特定區域，在前段製程中主動形成反向拉扯的「對沖」應⼒，將基板維持
在平整狀態。
When the market started to use balance ﬁlm?
我們需要了解，⼤型  AI GPU/TPU 晶⽚  的  CoW 矽 / 玻璃中介層「有翹曲問
題」不代表現在都⼀定會⽤獨⽴的  Balance Film 。  Balance Film 是⼀種額
外的「反向應⼒補償材料」，主要在⼤尺⼨、⾼層數、薄型化、 RDL/PLP/ 先進
封裝製程翹曲變嚴重時導⼊，⽽不是標準必備層。
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Why a larger AI chip needs it?
Chip size matters
AI GPU/TPU 的矽中介層  Silicon Interposer 將⾛到 5.5x 光罩尺⼨，晶⽚⾯
積增加後，當台積電 AI GPU 客戶 Nvidia/AMD 及 AI ASIC/TPU 客戶亞⾺遜 / ⾕
歌 /Meta/ 微軟為了追求更⾼算⼒從 2028 年開始推疊邏輯電晶體及 12 顆

HBM4E 存儲記憶體，甚⾄ 20-24 顆 HBM5E 存儲記憶體，⼀⽚ 12“ 晶圓能切出
8 ⽚的 5.5x reticle size (70mmx70mm=4,900mm2) 晶⽚⼤⼩所需要的矽
中介層（ Silicon Interposer) ，將只能切出 5 ⽚  9x reticle size (90mm x

### Page 3

90mm=8,100mm2) 晶⽚⼤⼩的矽中介層，甚⾄ 4 ⽚  14x reticle size
(110mm x 110mm=12,100mm2) 的矽中介層，這意思是指假設台積電客
戶全⾯推出新⼀代  9x/14x reticle size 的晶⽚產品，即使數量不變，台積電
矽中介層的產能會⽴刻減少 40-50% ，如果再考量良率因素，矽中介層產能減
少 50% 或以上， 9x/14x reticle size 的矽中介層漲價⼀倍是⾮常合理的。因
為這個結構性變化，我們認為台積電對於幫助⼤晶⽚客戶採⽤玻璃中介層取代
矽中介層，於 2029 年由圓轉⽅加速擴張 CoPoS 產能及量產  CoPoS 將是台積
電未來 10 年不得不為的重⼤決定（請參考我們於 2026 年 3 ⽉ 27 ⽇發出的深度
報告：CoWoS/FOWLP 轉  CoPoS/FOPLP 圓轉⽅的典範轉移及產業鏈分析）。
現⾏⼤型  AI ABF substrate 已普遍需要  low-CTE core 、 symmetrical

stack-up 等⼿段抑制  warpage 。當矽 / 玻璃中介層未來超過  90mm x
90mm=8,100mm2 ⼤⼩，矽晶⽚、矽 / 玻璃中介層，銅（ RDL ）、與環氧樹
脂（ EMC ）之間的熱膨脹係數（ CTE ）差異，會導致翹曲（ Warpage ）呈現
「⾮線性」的急遽惡化。當翹曲變形量超過微影設備的對焦容許度（ Depth of
Focus ）或造成晶圓 / ⾯板破裂時，傳統的物理載具已無法壓制，就必須在製程
前段導⼊  Balance Film 。
Source: TSMC
付費訂閱戶可分享  Part I 全⽂，為何 AI ⼤尺⼨晶⽚需
要 BF(Size, layers, mix matters) ，貼在哪裡，是半
導體產品材料還是製程耗材？ Balance ﬁlm 移除後如
何防翹曲？ Part II 重點
Subscribed
Layers matter for ABF substrate

### Page 4

AI GPU/TPU I/O 數量暴增，因此  ABF 載板的  routing layer 越來越多。 ABF
載板本⾝是  build-up dielectric ，以後⾼階  ABF 載板  可達超過  20+
layers 。

Bottom 底端
⽬前理想狀態是上下完全對稱，內部應⼒互相抵消，但實際  AI ABF 載板，因
為不同的  power plane, routing density, copper density,
interposer/RDL 只在⼀側， HBM/GPU/TPU 集中在頂端不可能永遠完全對
稱， molding/underﬁll/TIM 熱介⾯材料（ TIM ， Thermal Interface
Material) 等材料不同，因此熱循環後會產⽣  bow / twist 。 Balance Film 的

作⽤就是⼈為加⼊⼀個相反⽅向的  stress ，使整體  stack 的  bending
moment 接近零。
Source: TSMC
Mixed chips matter
未來的 AI GPU/TPU 已經從單純  AI 加速器  + ABF substrate 變成  AI 加速器
+ CPO/Optics on 中介層  (EIC/PIC/FAU) + 矽 / 玻璃中介層  + HBM 的超級
複合體，⽽每種材料的熱膨脹係數都不同：

### Page 5

- 矽：約  2.6 ppm/K
- 銅：約  16.5-17.0 ppm/K
- 玻璃：約 0.5-10 ppm/k
- ABF 載板：平⾯⽅向  X-Y 軸  12-16 ppm/K ；垂直⽅向  Z 軸  30-50 ppm/K
所以從室溫到  reﬂow 250°C 左右，應⼒差⾮常⼤。 Ajinomoto 也把  low
CTE / high Tg 列為最新  ABF 材料的重要特性，⽬的之⼀就是控制熱機械變
形。
Source: TSMC
Where balance ﬁlm attached to?
Balance Film 不是留在最終晶⽚產品的材料，更像是製程耗材，核⼼概念是
「反向對沖」。因為封裝結構的正⾯（ Front-side ）堆疊了⼤量會產⽣應⼒的
材料，所以  Balance Film 通常是貼附在矽 / 玻璃中介層或玻璃載體  glass
carrier 的背⾯（未來也有機會⽤在  ABF 載板 ) ，並不等於 CoPoS 的專屬材料
（請參考之前圓 CoWoS 轉⽅ CoPoS 深度報告）。

根據不同的製程階段，其貼合位置與作⽤對象略有不同：
1. RDL （重佈線層）製程階段
貼合位置：  玻璃載體或矽 / 玻璃中介層的最底層背⾯，並可能隨著 RDL 處理層
數增加⽽多次使⽤。當  Silicon Interposer 經過背⾯研磨薄化⾄  50–100 微
⽶時，結構已無法⾃我⽀撐，必須暫時鍵合在玻璃載具（ Glass Carrier ）
上，請參考下圖。此時， Balance Film 就會改貼在「玻璃載具的最下⽅」，防
⽌巨型載板在後續製程中因上下⼒矩失衡⽽碎裂。

### Page 6

作⽤機制：  在載板正⾯不斷塗佈  PI/PBO 介電層與電鍍厚銅線路時，這些材料
在⾼溫固化與冷卻後會產⽣強⼤的收縮⼒，導致載板向上捲曲（ Smile 變形）
或向下凸起（ Cry 變形）。貼在背⾯的  Balance Film 會透過⾃⾝特製的⾼分
⼦材料特性，在同溫層產⽣「反向的拉扯應⼒」，將載板強⾏拉平，確保後續
⿈光微影製程的精準對位。
Source: TrendForce
2. Bump & Reﬂow （凸塊與迴銲）製程階段
貼合位置：  環氧樹脂（ EMC ）載體的背⾯。
作⽤機制：  當進⾏  Chip-ﬁrst 或  Chip-last 製程進⼊⾼溫迴銲爐時， EMC 與
晶⽚的膨脹率截然不同。此時  Balance Film 作為暫時性的應⼒平衡層貼合於
背⾯，可抵消⾼溫帶來的劇烈形變，防⽌晶⽚移位或焊點斷裂。
總結來說：  Balance Film 就像是拔河⽐賽中的另⼀⽅。正⾯（晶⽚與線路
端）有多⼤的⼒量拉扯導致基板變形，背⾯的  Balance Film 就必須透過精準

計算的材料特性（如模數、 CTE 等），施加同等的⼒量將其拉回⽔平。它是⼀
種具有功能性的暫時犧牲層，通常在製程完成、應⼒釋放或結構固定後，會透
過解膠（ De-taping ）程序移除。

### Page 7

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Avoiding warpage to return
Balance Film 的主要⽬的不是「永久消除應⼒」，⽽是搶救製程良率。微影機
台的曝光對焦景深（ DOF ）通常只有數個微⽶的容忍度，只要基板翹曲超過極
限，光阻就會失焦、⾛線就會斷裂。 Balance Film 在關鍵幾道⾼溫化學品與
電鍍⼯站維持住絕對⽔平，其任務就算圓滿完成。
雖然從產業鏈了解，⽬前台積電交給專做  CoS chip on substrate 封測廠的
CoW AI GPU/TPU 在出廠時即使不使⽤  Balance ﬁlm 仍保持平整（部分原
因是矽中介層還沒超過 9 個光罩尺⼨），但時間⼀拉⻑，如果不貼上  Balance
ﬁlm ，也沒有其他結構接⼿，殘留應⼒必然會導致翹曲「彈性回跳」
（ Springback ）。所以台積電希望  CoS 封測廠能共同分攤 AI ⼤晶⽚  Balance
ﬁlm 隨產品出貨的成本。但在實際先進封裝產線中，翹曲之所以不會毀掉成

品，是因為隨產品出貨的  Balance Film 的核⼼使命是「階段性保駕護航」
—— 它確保基板在最脆弱的微影與線路成形階段維持平整，⼀旦進⼊後段，應
⼒的承載就會透過以下機制順利交棒：
1. 結構⽀撐接⼒（ Structural Handoﬀ ）
Balance Film 不會在基板毫無⽀撐的裸露狀態下被撕除。
通常是在晶圓 / ⾯板完成正⾯多層  RDL 製作後，先將正⾯與剛性載具（如
第⼆層暫時載板、 Bumping 載具）或次世代⽬標基板（如  ABF 載板）
完成貼合或鍵合（ Bonding ），隨後才將背⾯的  Balance Film 解膠剝
離。這時，結構的剛性已經由下游的承載體接⼿。
2. 加強框與散熱蓋物理鎖定（ Stiﬀener Ring & Lid ）
對於尺⼨超過  80mm × 80mm 以上的⼤型  AI 晶⽚，封裝完成移除製
程保護層後，本體仍有強烈的翹曲趨勢。

### Page 8

後段製程會⽴刻在封裝邊緣環氧樹脂表⾯貼上⾦屬加強框（ Stiﬀener
Ring ），或是封上均熱⽚ / 散熱蓋（ Lid ），利⽤⾦屬件極⾼的機械剛性，直
接將整個封裝體強制「鎖平」，避免在後續  SMT 焊接到主機板時發⽣焊
點虛焊或開裂。
3. 對稱性結構補償（ Symmetric Stack-up Design ）
Balance Film 解決的是「製程中單側堆疊引發的暫態應⼒失衡」。
在產品的終態設計中，⼯程師會在基板的上下兩側設計對稱的介電層
（ Dielectric Layer ）與等效銅箔⾛線密度（ Copper Ratio
Balancing ）。當封裝整體完成、所有⾼分⼦材料完全熟化（ Cure ）後，
本體正反⾯的⼒矩會互相抵消，使結構達到⾃發性的⼒學平衡（ Self-
balancing ）。
Story to be continued
在透過更多產業調研之後，我們之後打算出  Part II ，來聊聊
有沒有更優的防翹曲技術？
產業鏈核⼼競爭⼒⽐較？
如何測算  Balance ﬁlm 的市場規模及對營收貢獻的影響？

## Document 346: US inflation, payrolls data support rate hike this week

Source PDF: all report pdf/US inflation, payrolls data support rate hike this week.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

PUBLIC 
US inflation, payrolls data 
support rate hike this week 
Summary 
Event: US core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, beating 
consensus expectations for a 0.2% rise. The inflation report 
follows a stronger-than-expected jobs report for August. The 
combination supports a Fed rate hike this week. However, 
annual core inflation eased to 2.4% y/y, from 2.5% , 
sustaining the disinflationary trend. Therefore, a Fed rate hike 
this week is likely to be one-off, not the start of a hiking cycle.  
Market reaction:  Bond and FX markets were relatively 
muted, while the S&P500 index jumped 0.9%. The policy rate 
sensitive US 2 -year yield rose 4bps to 4.63% as markets 
factored in almost 90% chance of rate hike this week. US 10-
year and 30-year yields were  little changed  at 4.97% and 
5.35%, respectively . The USD index (DXY) rose 0. 1%, 
staying close to its 200-day moving average. 
Investment strategy: The latest inflation report for August 
reinforces the case for a rate hike at this week's Fed policy 
meeting. As signalled in our previous CIO publications , 
“Rising Risk of a Fed Rate Hike” and “Inching towards a Fed 
‘credibility’ hike”, we now believe there is 90% chance that 
the Fed will raise the Fed Funds Rate in September.  
We view this more as a “credibility” hike by the Fed due to 
Chair Warsh’s hawkish rhetoric at the Fed’s recent annual 
Jackson Hole summit. US c ore consumer inflation , while 
hotter than expected, continued to move in the right direction 
on an annualised basis, easing to 2.4% y/y, from 2.5%. Also, 
US wage growth continues to slow, as reflected in the latest 
average hourly earnings. As such we do not believe this is 
the beginning of a cycle of multiple rate hikes by the Fed.  
Risks to the view:  Headline inflation accelerated to 0.4% 
m/m and stayed elevated at +3.4% y/y. Energy prices, driven 
by the Middle East conflict, were a primary contributor, with 
oil prices having surged in the days preceding the print. The 
producer inflation report released last week also showed 
producer prices rising 0.4% m/m, the most since May, and 
5.4% y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
 US stocks rose, while bond yields and the USD were 
little changed, after a slightly hotter-than-expected 
inflation report cemented the chance of a Fed rate hike 
Asset performance: 10-Sep close to 11-Sep close

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

US core inflation continued to ease in August, despite a 
m/m acceleration. This argues for one rate hike, not the 
start of a multiple rate hiking cycle
